A little, yes… I mean, I try not to make a big deal of it. But before I leave for work or if I have a meeting, I’ll go back to the bathroom mirror a few times and fix my collar or my hair around my neck. Then I might check it again on my phone camera, just to make sure it doesn’t look odd.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I’m here because I’ve been really bothered by how my jaw and neck look. I just can’t seem to stop thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over to try to make your jaw and neck look better?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I try not to make a big deal of it. It’s mostly this one thing that brought me in—the sagging, uneven area around my jaw and neck.\n\nEven when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s what families at work, nurses, or people at church notice first. I don’t want to say people are judging me overall, because that sounds maybe too strong. It’s more that I can’t stop noticing that sagging, uneven spot, and then I feel like when families at work, or the nurses, or people at church look at me, that’s the first thing they see. Even if they tell me it looks normal, I still feel like they notice it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people would judge you less harshly or notice you less if you weren't worried about the appearance of your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe, yes. I don’t think people are being harsh on purpose or anything.     "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

If it were completely covered… I think I’d be less worried, yes. Not that I think everyone is sitting there judging me, I don’t mean it like that.

It’s just, I came in because I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven place around my jaw and neck. And when it’s visible, I feel like that’s the first thing families at work, the nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me, even when they say it looks normal. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I’m here because I’ve been really bothered by how my jaw and neck look. There’s this sagging, uneven spot that I keep seeing, and I know people tell me it looks normal, so maybe I’m making too much of it… but it feels like it’s the first thing people notice when they look at me—families I work with, nurses, people at church. I just can’t seem to stop thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over to try to make your jaw and neck look better?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I try not to make a big deal of it. But before I leave for work or if I have a meeting, I’ll go back to the bathroom mirror a few times and fix my collar or my hair around my neck. Then I might check it again on my phone camera, just to make sure it doesn’t look odd.\n\nSometimes I’ll ask my daughter, like, “Does this look strange?” I know that probably sounds silly… but I just feel better if she says it’s okay. It has made me late more than once, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checking behaviors or else something bad will happen?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say something bad exactly… not like a disaster. It’s more that I feel really uneasy if I don’t check. Like before work or a meeting, I’ll keep going back to the bathroom mirror, fixing my collar or my hair around my neck, then maybe look again on my phone camera.\n\nAnd sometimes I’ll ask my daughter if it looks strange. I know that’s probably not the best thing to do, and I’m sorry if it sounds excessive… but if I don’t do it, I just feel like I can’t go out feeling okay. It’s made me late more than once.\n\nDoctor: Is it just your jaw and neck that you're concerned about, or are there other parts of your body that you worry about as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the jaw and neck, yes. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but that’s the thing I just keep noticing… that sagging, uneven spot there. Even when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s what families at work, nurses, or people at church are seeing first when they look at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be worried about how you look if someone were able to completely fix the sagging, uneven spot on your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think if it were fixed, I’d stop worrying so much… I mean, that’s what I tell myself. The reason I came in is really because I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck.\n\nEven when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families at work, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me. So… maybe if that part were completely fixed, I’d feel better. I’m not sure. I just know that’s the thing I keep getting stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other parts of your body that you've worried about in the past, even if you're not worried about them now?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m always picking myself apart. It’s mostly this one thing that brought me in—the sagging, uneven area around my jaw and neck.\n\nEven when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s what families at work, nurses, or people at church notice first. That’s the part I can’t seem to stop seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are judging you negatively in general, or is it mostly about this specific area of your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly that specific area… my jaw and neck. I don’t want to say people are judging me overall, because that sounds maybe too strong. It’s more that I can’t stop noticing that sagging, uneven spot, and then I feel like when families at work, or the nurses, or people at church look at me, that’s the first thing they see. Even if they tell me it looks normal, I still feel like they notice it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people would judge you less harshly or notice you less if you weren't worried about the appearance of your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe, yes. I don’t think people are being harsh on purpose or anything. I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nIt’s more that I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck, and then I feel like everyone else must be seeing it too—families at work, nurses, people at church. Even when they say it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing they notice. So if I weren’t worried about it… maybe I’d feel like they noticed me less in that way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be worried about people judging you if you were in a situation where your jaw and neck were completely covered?\n\nPatient: If it were completely covered… I think I’d be less worried, yes. Not that I think everyone is sitting there judging me, I don’t mean it like that.\n\nIt’s just, I came in because I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven place around my jaw and neck. And when it’s visible, I feel like that’s the first thing families at work, the nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me, even when they say it looks normal. If they couldn’t see it at all, I think I’d feel a little safer, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worry about how you look would disappear completely if you could change just your jaw and neck to look exactly how you want them to?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would, honestly. If my jaw and neck looked the way I want, I think I’d feel a lot better.\n\nBut… I don’t know if I can say it would disappear completely. The main thing is that I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot there, and even when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families at work, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me. So changing that would probably help a lot… I just don’t want to overstate it. Is that okay to say?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. There’s this sagging, uneven spot that I keep seeing, and I know people tell me it looks normal, so maybe I’m making too much of it… but it feels like it’s the first thing people notice when they look at me—families I work with, nurses, people at church. I just can’t seem to stop thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over to try to make your jaw and neck look better?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I try not to make a big deal of it. But before I leave for work or if I have a meeting, I’ll go back to the bathroom mirror a few times and fix my collar or my hair around my neck. Then I might check it again on my phone camera, just to make sure it doesn’t look odd.\n\nSometimes I’ll ask my daughter, like, “Does this look strange?” I know that probably sounds silly… but I just feel better if she says it’s okay. It has made me late more than once, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checking behaviors or else something bad will happen?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say something bad exactly… not like a disaster. It’s more that I feel really uneasy if I don’t check. Like before work or a meeting, I’ll keep going back to the bathroom mirror, fixing my collar or my hair around my neck, then maybe look again on my phone camera.\n\nAnd sometimes I’ll ask my daughter if it looks strange. I know that’s probably not the best thing to do, and I’m sorry if it sounds excessive… but if I don’t do it, I just feel like I can’t go out feeling okay. It’s made me late more than once.\n\nDoctor: Is it just your jaw and neck that you're concerned about, or are there other parts of your body that you worry about as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the jaw and neck, yes. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but that’s the thing I just keep noticing… that sagging, uneven spot there. Even when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s what families at work, nurses, or people at church are seeing first when they look at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be worried about how you look if someone were able to completely fix the sagging, uneven spot on your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think if it were fixed, I’d stop worrying so much… I mean, that’s what I tell myself. The reason I came in is really because I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck.\n\nEven when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families at work, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me. So… maybe if that part were completely fixed, I’d feel better. I’m not sure. I just know that’s the thing I keep getting stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other parts of your body that you've worried about in the past, even if you're not worried about them now?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m always picking myself apart. It’s mostly this one thing that brought me in—the sagging, uneven area around my jaw and neck.\n\nEven when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s what families at work, nurses, or people at church notice first. That’s the part I can’t seem to stop seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are judging you negatively in general, or is it mostly about this specific area of your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly that specific area… my jaw and neck. I don’t want to say people are judging me overall, because that sounds maybe too strong. It’s more that I can’t stop noticing that sagging, uneven spot, and then I feel like when families at work, or the nurses, or people at church look at me, that’s the first thing they see. Even if they tell me it looks normal, I still feel like they notice it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people would judge you less harshly or notice you less if you weren't worried about the appearance of your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe, yes. I don’t think people are being harsh on purpose or anything. I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nIt’s more that I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck, and then I feel like everyone else must be seeing it too—families at work, nurses, people at church. Even when they say it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing they notice. So if I weren’t worried about it… maybe I’d feel like they noticed me less in that way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be worried about people judging you if you were in a situation where your jaw and neck were completely covered?\n\nPatient: If it were completely covered… I think I’d be less worried, yes. Not that I think everyone is sitting there judging me, I don’t mean it like that.\n\nIt’s just, I came in because I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven place around my jaw and neck. And when it’s visible, I feel like that’s the first thing families at work, the nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me, even when they say it looks normal. If they couldn’t see it at all, I think I’d feel a little safer, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worry about how you look would disappear completely if you could change just your jaw and neck to look exactly how you want them to?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would, honestly. If my jaw and neck looked the way I want, I think I’d feel a lot better.\n\nBut… I don’t know if I can say it would disappear completely. The main thing is that I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot there, and even when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families at work, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me. So changing that would probably help a lot… I just don’t want to overstate it. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would worry about what other people think of you if you didn't have this concern about your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I think… probably not as much. I mean, I’d still care what people think of me, like anyone does, but this feels very tied to that one thing.\n\nI came in because I just can’t stop seeing this sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck. And even when folks tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me. So if I didn’t have that concern, I think I’d be a lot less worried about being judged. Is that answering what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about being judged or evaluated by others in situations where your appearance is not the main focus?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, if my appearance isn’t really involved, I don’t think I worry about it in the same way.\n\nI might still want to do a good job or not come across badly, like at work or talking to people, but I don’t know that I’d call it the same kind of worry. It’s more when I feel like they can see me—see that area—that I get caught up in feeling judged. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about what other people think of your personality or behaviors, separate from how you look?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way. I mean, I sometimes wonder if I said something awkward or if I’m being polite enough, but I think that’s pretty normal.\n\nIt doesn’t feel like the main worry for me. The thing that really gets stuck in my head is more about how I look, not whether people dislike my personality or what I’m doing. Does that answer it okay?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Like before work or a meeting, I’ll keep going back to the bathroom mirror, fixing my collar or my hair around my neck, then maybe look again on my phone camera.\n\nAnd sometimes I’ll ask my daughter if it looks strange. I know that’s probably not the best thing to do, and I’m sorry if it sounds excessive… but if I don’t do it, I just feel like I can’t go out feeling okay. It’s made me late more than once.\n\nDoctor: Is it just your jaw and neck that you're concerned about, or are there other parts of your body that you worry about as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the jaw and neck, yes. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but that’s the thing I just keep noticing… that sagging, uneven spot there. Even when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s what families at work, nurses, or people at church are seeing first when they look at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be worried about how you look if someone were able to completely fix the sagging, uneven spot on your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think if it were fixed, I’d stop worrying so much… I mean, that’s what I tell myself. The reason I came in is really because I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck.\n\nEven when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families at work, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me. So… maybe if that part were completely fixed, I’d feel better. I’m not sure. I just know that’s the thing I keep getting stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other parts of your body that you've worried about in the past, even if you're not worried about them now?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m always picking myself apart. It’s mostly this one thing that brought me in—the sagging, uneven area around my jaw and neck.\n\nEven when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s what families at work, nurses, or people at church notice first. That’s the part I can’t seem to stop seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are judging you negatively in general, or is it mostly about this specific area of your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly that specific area… my jaw and neck. I don’t want to say people are judging me overall, because that sounds maybe too strong. It’s more that I can’t stop noticing that sagging, uneven spot, and then I feel like when families at work, or the nurses, or people at church look at me, that’s the first thing they see. Even if they tell me it looks normal, I still feel like they notice it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people would judge you less harshly or notice you less if you weren't worried about the appearance of your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe, yes. I don’t think people are being harsh on purpose or anything. I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nIt’s more that I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck, and then I feel like everyone else must be seeing it too—families at work, nurses, people at church. Even when they say it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing they notice. So if I weren’t worried about it… maybe I’d feel like they noticed me less in that way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be worried about people judging you if you were in a situation where your jaw and neck were completely covered?\n\nPatient: If it were completely covered… I think I’d be less worried, yes. Not that I think everyone is sitting there judging me, I don’t mean it like that.\n\nIt’s just, I came in because I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven place around my jaw and neck. And when it’s visible, I feel like that’s the first thing families at work, the nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me, even when they say it looks normal. If they couldn’t see it at all, I think I’d feel a little safer, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worry about how you look would disappear completely if you could change just your jaw and neck to look exactly how you want them to?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would, honestly. If my jaw and neck looked the way I want, I think I’d feel a lot better.\n\nBut… I don’t know if I can say it would disappear completely. The main thing is that I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot there, and even when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families at work, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me. So changing that would probably help a lot… I just don’t want to overstate it. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would worry about what other people think of you if you didn't have this concern about your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I think… probably not as much. I mean, I’d still care what people think of me, like anyone does, but this feels very tied to that one thing.\n\nI came in because I just can’t stop seeing this sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck. And even when folks tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me. So if I didn’t have that concern, I think I’d be a lot less worried about being judged. Is that answering what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about being judged or evaluated by others in situations where your appearance is not the main focus?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, if my appearance isn’t really involved, I don’t think I worry about it in the same way.\n\nI might still want to do a good job or not come across badly, like at work or talking to people, but I don’t know that I’d call it the same kind of worry. It’s more when I feel like they can see me—see that area—that I get caught up in feeling judged. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about what other people think of your personality or behaviors, separate from how you look?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way. I mean, I sometimes wonder if I said something awkward or if I’m being polite enough, but I think that’s pretty normal.\n\nIt doesn’t feel like the main worry for me. The thing that really gets stuck in my head is more about how I look, not whether people dislike my personality or what I’m doing. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still worry about being imperfect or having flaws if they were not related to how you look?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I don’t love making mistakes, nobody does, but I don’t sit around worrying about being flawed in general.\n\nIt’s mostly when it’s connected to how I look that it starts feeling hard to let go of. If it wasn’t about that, I think I’d be able to move past things more normally. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Like before work or a meeting, I’ll keep going back to the bathroom mirror, fixing my collar or my hair around my neck, then maybe look again on my phone camera.\n\nAnd sometimes I’ll ask my daughter if it looks strange. I know that’s probably not the best thing to do, and I’m sorry if it sounds excessive… but if I don’t do it, I just feel like I can’t go out feeling okay. It’s made me late more than once.\n\nDoctor: Is it just your jaw and neck that you're concerned about, or are there other parts of your body that you worry about as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s the jaw and neck, yes. I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but that’s the thing I just keep noticing… that sagging, uneven spot there. Even when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s what families at work, nurses, or people at church are seeing first when they look at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be worried about how you look if someone were able to completely fix the sagging, uneven spot on your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think if it were fixed, I’d stop worrying so much… I mean, that’s what I tell myself. The reason I came in is really because I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck.\n\nEven when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families at work, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me. So… maybe if that part were completely fixed, I’d feel better. I’m not sure. I just know that’s the thing I keep getting stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other parts of your body that you've worried about in the past, even if you're not worried about them now?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t think so. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m always picking myself apart. It’s mostly this one thing that brought me in—the sagging, uneven area around my jaw and neck.\n\nEven when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s what families at work, nurses, or people at church notice first. That’s the part I can’t seem to stop seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are judging you negatively in general, or is it mostly about this specific area of your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly that specific area… my jaw and neck. I don’t want to say people are judging me overall, because that sounds maybe too strong. It’s more that I can’t stop noticing that sagging, uneven spot, and then I feel like when families at work, or the nurses, or people at church look at me, that’s the first thing they see. Even if they tell me it looks normal, I still feel like they notice it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people would judge you less harshly or notice you less if you weren't worried about the appearance of your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe, yes. I don’t think people are being harsh on purpose or anything. I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nIt’s more that I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck, and then I feel like everyone else must be seeing it too—families at work, nurses, people at church. Even when they say it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing they notice. So if I weren’t worried about it… maybe I’d feel like they noticed me less in that way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be worried about people judging you if you were in a situation where your jaw and neck were completely covered?\n\nPatient: If it were completely covered… I think I’d be less worried, yes. Not that I think everyone is sitting there judging me, I don’t mean it like that.\n\nIt’s just, I came in because I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven place around my jaw and neck. And when it’s visible, I feel like that’s the first thing families at work, the nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me, even when they say it looks normal. If they couldn’t see it at all, I think I’d feel a little safer, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worry about how you look would disappear completely if you could change just your jaw and neck to look exactly how you want them to?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would, honestly. If my jaw and neck looked the way I want, I think I’d feel a lot better.\n\nBut… I don’t know if I can say it would disappear completely. The main thing is that I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot there, and even when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families at work, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me. So changing that would probably help a lot… I just don’t want to overstate it. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would worry about what other people think of you if you didn't have this concern about your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I think… probably not as much. I mean, I’d still care what people think of me, like anyone does, but this feels very tied to that one thing.\n\nI came in because I just can’t stop seeing this sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck. And even when folks tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me. So if I didn’t have that concern, I think I’d be a lot less worried about being judged. Is that answering what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about being judged or evaluated by others in situations where your appearance is not the main focus?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, if my appearance isn’t really involved, I don’t think I worry about it in the same way.\n\nI might still want to do a good job or not come across badly, like at work or talking to people, but I don’t know that I’d call it the same kind of worry. It’s more when I feel like they can see me—see that area—that I get caught up in feeling judged. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about what other people think of your personality or behaviors, separate from how you look?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way. I mean, I sometimes wonder if I said something awkward or if I’m being polite enough, but I think that’s pretty normal.\n\nIt doesn’t feel like the main worry for me. The thing that really gets stuck in my head is more about how I look, not whether people dislike my personality or what I’m doing. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still worry about being imperfect or having flaws if they were not related to how you look?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I don’t love making mistakes, nobody does, but I don’t sit around worrying about being flawed in general.\n\nIt’s mostly when it’s connected to how I look that it starts feeling hard to let go of. If it wasn’t about that, I think I’d be able to move past things more normally. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel anxious or uneasy if you were in a situation where you couldn't see yourself, but others could?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I would. Maybe not if I could just forget about it, but I usually can’t.\n\nEven if I’m not seeing myself, I’m still thinking they can see that sagging, uneven part around my jaw and neck. Like families at work, or nurses, or people at church—I feel like it’s the first thing they notice when they look at me, even when people tell me it looks normal.\n\nI know that probably sounds a little much, but that’s honestly why I came in. It’s hard to get it out of my mind.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess I’m here because I’ve been really bothered by how my jaw and neck look. There’s this sagging, uneven spot that I keep seeing, and I know people tell me it looks normal, so maybe I’m making too much of it… but it feels like it’s the first thing people notice when they look at me—families I work with, nurses, people at church. I just can’t seem to stop thinking about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself doing anything over and over to try to make your jaw and neck look better?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I try not to make a big deal of it. I know that’s probably not the best thing to do, and I’m sorry if it sounds excessive… but if I don’t do it, I just feel like I can’t go out feeling okay. Even when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s what families at work, nurses, or people at church are seeing first when they look at me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be worried about how you look if someone were able to completely fix the sagging, uneven spot on your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think if it were fixed, I’d stop worrying so much… I mean, that’s what I tell myself. The reason I came in is really because I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck.\n\nEven when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families at work, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m always picking myself apart. It’s mostly this one thing that brought me in—the sagging, uneven area around my jaw and neck.\n\nEven when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s what families at work, nurses, or people at church notice first. That’s the part I can’t seem to stop seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are judging you negatively in general, or is it mostly about this specific area of your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly that specific area… my jaw and neck. I don’t want to say people are judging me overall, because that sounds maybe too strong. It’s more that I can’t stop noticing that sagging, uneven spot, and then I feel like when families at work, or the nurses, or people at church look at me, that’s the first thing they see. Even if they tell me it looks normal, I still feel like they notice it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people would judge you less harshly or notice you less if you weren't worried about the appearance of your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I guess… maybe, yes. I don’t think people are being harsh on purpose or anything. I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nIt’s more that I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck, and then I feel like everyone else must be seeing it too—families at work, nurses, people at church. Even when they say it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing they notice. So if I weren’t worried about it… maybe I’d feel like they noticed me less in that way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be worried about people judging you if you were in a situation where your jaw and neck were completely covered?\n\nPatient: If it were completely covered… I think I’d be less worried, yes. Not that I think everyone is sitting there judging me, I don’t mean it like that.\n\nIt’s just, I came in because I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven place around my jaw and neck. And when it’s visible, I feel like that’s the first thing families at work, the nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me, even when they say it looks normal. If they couldn’t see it at all, I think I’d feel a little safer, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worry about how you look would disappear completely if you could change just your jaw and neck to look exactly how you want them to?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would, honestly. If my jaw and neck looked the way I want, I think I’d feel a lot better.\n\nBut… I don’t know if I can say it would disappear completely. The main thing is that I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot there, and even when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families at work, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me. So changing that would probably help a lot… I just don’t want to overstate it. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would worry about what other people think of you if you didn't have this concern about your jaw and neck?\n\nPatient: I think… probably not as much. And even when folks tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me. So if I didn’t have that concern, I think I’d be a lot less worried about being judged. Is that answering what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about being judged or evaluated by others in situations where your appearance is not the main focus?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, if my appearance isn’t really involved, I don’t think I worry about it in the same way.\n\nI might still want to do a good job or not come across badly, like at work or talking to people, but I don’t know that I’d call it the same kind of worry. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about what other people think of your personality or behaviors, separate from how you look?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way. I mean, I sometimes wonder if I said something awkward or if I’m being polite enough, but I think that’s pretty normal.\n\nIt doesn’t feel like the main worry for me. The thing that really gets stuck in my head is more about how I look, not whether people dislike my personality or what I’m doing. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still worry about being imperfect or having flaws if they were not related to how you look?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I don’t love making mistakes, nobody does, but I don’t sit around worrying about being flawed in general.\n\nIt’s mostly when it’s connected to how I look that it starts feeling hard to let go of. If it wasn’t about that, I think I’d be able to move past things more normally. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel anxious or uneasy if you were in a situation where you couldn't see yourself, but others could?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I would. Maybe not if I could just forget about it, but I usually can’t.\n\nEven if I’m not seeing myself, I’m still thinking they can see that sagging, uneven part around my jaw and neck. Like families at work, or nurses, or people at church—I feel like it’s the first thing they notice when they look at me, even when people tell me it looks normal.\n\nI know that probably sounds a little much, but that’s honestly why I came in. It’s hard to get it out of my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel anxious or uneasy about social interactions if your appearance was not a factor?\n\nPatient: I think… if my appearance really wasn’t part of it, I probably wouldn’t be nearly as anxious. I mean, I can be a little shy sometimes, but that’s not what brought me here.\n\nIt’s more that I can’t stop noticing this sagging, uneven place around my jaw and neck, and then I assume other people see it too—families at work, nurses, people at church. I don’t want to make it sound like that.\n\nIt’s more that I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck, and then I feel like everyone else must be seeing it too—families at work, nurses, people at church. Even when they say it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing they notice. So if I weren’t worried about it… maybe I’d feel like they noticed me less in that way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be worried about people judging you if you were in a situation where your jaw and neck were completely covered?\n\nPatient: If it were completely covered… I think I’d be less worried, yes. Not that I think everyone is sitting there judging me, I don’t mean it like that.\n\nIt’s just, I came in because I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven place around my jaw and neck. And when it’s visible, I feel like that’s the first thing families at work, the nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me, even when they say it looks normal. If they couldn’t see it at all, I think I’d feel a little safer, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worry about how you look would disappear completely if you could change just your jaw and neck to look exactly how you want them to?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would, honestly. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think… if there truly wasn’t anything I could change, I’d like to say I’d accept it and not worry so much. But honestly, I think I’d still feel uneasy, because I keep noticing that sagging, uneven area around my jaw and neck.

Even when people tell me it looks normal, I have a hard time believing it. I still feel like families at work, or nurses, or people at church are seeing it right away when they look at me.

I’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is… that’s just what keeps bothering me. Does that make sense?

If they couldn’t see it at all, I think I’d feel a little safer, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worry about how you look would disappear completely if you could change just your jaw and neck to look exactly how you want them to?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think it would, honestly. I think if I could completely ignore my appearance, then… yes, a lot of that anxiety would probably go away.

I don’t mean to make it sound like I’m obsessed in a dramatic way, but that’s why I came in. I keep seeing this sagging, uneven place around my jaw and neck, and even when people say it looks normal, I still feel like families at work, or nurses, or people at church notice it right away.

Is that answering what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about being judged or evaluated by others in situations where your appearance is not the main focus?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, if my appearance isn’t really involved, I don’t think I worry about it in the same way.\n\nI might still want to do a good job or not come across badly, like at work or talking to people, but I don’t know that I’d call it the same kind of worry. It’s more when I feel like they can see me—see that area—that I get caught up in feeling judged. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about what other people think of your personality or behaviors, separate from how you look?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a big way. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still worry about being imperfect or having flaws if they were not related to how you look?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. I mean, I don’t love making mistakes, nobody does, but I don’t sit around worrying about being flawed in general.\n\nIt’s mostly when it’s connected to how I look that it starts feeling hard to let go of. If it wasn’t about that, I think I’d be able to move past things more normally. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel anxious or uneasy if you were in a situation where you couldn't see yourself, but others could?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I would. Maybe not if I could just forget about it, but I usually can’t.\n\nEven if I’m not seeing myself, I’m still thinking they can see that sagging, uneven part around my jaw and neck. Like families at work, or nurses, or people at church—I feel like it’s the first thing they notice when they look at me, even when people tell me it looks normal.\n\nI know that probably sounds a little much, but that’s honestly why I came in. It’s hard to get it out of my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel anxious or uneasy about social interactions if your appearance was not a factor?\n\nPatient: I think… if my appearance really wasn’t part of it, I probably wouldn’t be nearly as anxious. I mean, I can be a little shy sometimes, but that’s not what brought me here.\n\nIt’s more that I can’t stop noticing this sagging, uneven place around my jaw and neck, and then I assume other people see it too—families at work, nurses, people at church. Even when they say it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing they notice.\n\nI’m sorry if I’m repeating myself. I just want to answer it right.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious in social situations if you were certain that nobody was judging your appearance?\n\nPatient: If I was truly certain nobody was judging how I looked… I think I’d be a lot less anxious, yes.\n\nI don’t want to sound like I’m blaming everything on that, but that really is why I came in. I can’t stop seeing that sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck, and even when people tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families at work, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me.\n\nSo if I really believed nobody was noticing or judging that, I think social situations would feel much easier. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious about yourself if you were in a situation where your appearance was completely irrelevant?\n\nPatient: If my appearance was really completely irrelevant… I think I’d feel a lot more okay with myself. Maybe not perfect, but much less anxious.\n\nI mean, the main reason I came in is because I can’t stop seeing this sagging, uneven spot around my jaw and neck. And even when folks tell me it looks normal, I still feel like it’s the first thing families, nurses, or people at church notice when they look at me.\n\nSo if that truly didn’t matter in the situation, I think I’d be calmer. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious about yourself if there was nothing about your appearance that you could change?\n\nPatient: I think… if there truly wasn’t anything I could change, I’d like to say I’d accept it and not worry so much. But honestly, I think I’d still feel uneasy, because I keep noticing that sagging, uneven area around my jaw and neck.\n\nEven when people tell me it looks normal, I have a hard time believing it. I still feel like families at work, or nurses, or people at church are seeing it right away when they look at me.\n\nI’m not trying to make it sound worse than it is… that’s just what keeps bothering me. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about yourself disappear if you were able to completely ignore your appearance?\n\nPatient: I think if I could completely ignore my appearance, then… yes, a lot of that anxiety would probably go away.\n\nI don’t mean to make it sound like I’m obsessed in a dramatic way, but that’s why I came in. I keep seeing this sagging, uneven place around my jaw and neck, and even when people say it looks normal, I still feel like families at work, or nurses, or people at church notice it right away.\n\nSo if I really could just not focus on how I look, I think I’d feel much calmer about myself. Is that the kind of thing you mean?"
